1|# CFR(6,21): mod-3 constant-character theorem accepted as a necessary condition
2|
3|Recorded at 2026-10-19T00:22:21-0700.
4|
5|**CORRECTNESS-ACCEPTED SCOPED PARTIAL (necessary condition only).** Theorem A, Corollary A1 and Corollary A2 hold as stated, read through errata 1 and 2. **CFR(6,21) remains OPEN.** Theorem A does not construct or exclude a seven-row cyclic Z_21 difference matrix. Priority and significance are unresolved; a focused primary screen is pending.
6|
7|## Accepted statements
8|
9|Let 3 | n and 9 ∤ n, and let D be any (Z_n,k;1) difference matrix with k ≥ 4. Reduce entries modulo 3. Call a coefficient vector χ on the rows translation-invariant if Σχ_i = 0.
10|
11|- **Theorem A.** If such a χ makes Σχ_i a_i(c) constant over all columns, then χ = 0 or |supp χ| ≥ 6.
12|- **Corollary A1 (k=7).** The space K of such constant characters has dimension at most one, so the mod-3 affine rank is 5 or 6. Rank 5 occurs only if K is spanned by one of three support-6/7 shapes up to sign and row relabelling. This applies to the seven-row matrix {0, τ_1,…,τ_6} associated with a CFR(6,n), τ_r = σ_r^−1, not to the six positional rows alone.
13|- **Corollary A2.** k=4,5 force full rank; k=6 forces rank at least 4. The same counting bound gives dim K ≤ 1 for every 4 ≤ k ≤ 7.
14|- **Consequence for constructions.** No seven-row cyclic Z_n difference matrix, with 3 | n and 9 ∤ n, satisfies any constant mod-3 relation Σχ_i a_i ≡ const with Σχ_i = 0 and 1 ≤ |supp χ| ≤ 5. Relations with nonzero coefficient sum are not excluded in general, because a column offset can create them.
15|
16|## Parent derivation
17|
18|Pair differences of a cyclic difference matrix are bijections onto Z_n; reducing modulo 3 makes every row difference balanced. For functions f,g on the columns, if f, g, f+g and f−g are balanced, each point (u,v) of F_3^2 receives exactly n/9 columns. The parent proved this by incidence rather than Fourier: the four lines through a point cover it four times and every other point once, so 4n/3 = n+3N(u,v). The [incidence control](cfr21-rank-parent-control-v1/incidence-control-v1.json) checks all nine points. A support-m sum-zero vector over F_3 with μ entries equal to −1 satisfies m+μ ≡ 0 (mod 3), giving the exhaustive support-3/4/5 shapes. The explicit pairs (f,g) in the proof make all four functions balanced, contradicting 9 ∤ n. A two-dimensional K would have total weight at most 6k but eight nonzero vectors of weight at least six, total at least 48, impossible for k ≤ 7. Row offsets, column offsets, units and permutations preserve K and the rank.
19|

Read together; no statement changed.
23|- [Codex review](/Users/jinleic/repos/aifours/institute/runs/six-lane-2026-10-18-96h/review/cfr21-mod3-rank-codex-v1.prompt.stdout.txt): ACCEPT-WITH-FIXES, observed gpt-6.1-sol, max, read-only. Its coefficient sum is2p+n_+−S(N), divisible by2p. Its length exceeds|Z| by L−(y+r)≥L+W−2p≥2, contradicting longestness. Thus S(N)≤slack; congruence and0≤n_+<2p force balance.

7|## Definitions and governing texts
8|
9|Y is a sequence of odd elements of C_(2p). (ZP_kappa) forbids zero-sum subsequences of even lengths from 2kappa+2 through 2p. Under this condition a long zero-sum has length at least 2p+2. Put kappa0=floor(2p/3), e=p-kappa. For the new-range LS/LS+/STAB claims below, p is prime >=7 and kappa0+1 <= kappa <= p-2 unless a narrower range is stated.
10|
11|Read the [delta package](/Users/jinleic/repos/aifours/mathematics/evidence/six-lane-20261018-96h/olson/continuations/stab-round2/delta-review-package-v1.md) through mandatory [AP-prime erratum](/Users/jinleic/repos/aifours/mathematics/evidence/six-lane-20261018-96h/olson/continuations/stab-round2/erratum-1-delta-APprime.md) and [consolidated delta erratum](/Users/jinleic/repos/aifours/mathematics/evidence/six-lane-20261018-96h/olson/continuations/stab-round2/erratum-2-delta-consolidated.md) `16cc18235f4353605ebd55753de4304bcf80e5b2c18b42f6fa0c68117795cc27`. 13|## Accepted results
14|
15|1. **LS-II'.** No threshold LS counterexample (odd Y of length p+2kappa+1, with (ZP_kappa) and a long zero-sum) lies in case (II) with respect to g=g0 of DGM-p. The former induction premise H'(kappa) and Lemma SH are not used; declared accepted imports still are.
16|2. **LS-small and Proposition I-small.** LS(p,p-2) for p>=7; LS(p,p-3) for p>=11; LS(p,p-4) for p>=13. Proposition I-small is accepted only with its own literal hypotheses and corrected AP-prime proof.
17|3. **BASE1 / BASE1+.** For p congruent to 1 mod3, p>=7, kappa=(2p+1)/3, an odd (ZP_kappa) sequence of length 3kappa+1=2p+2 containing a long zero-sum is exactly
18|   - g^(p+3)(3g)^(p-1), or
19|   - g^(2p-1)(cg)^3, where c=(2p+1)/3=3^(-1) mod2p,
20|   for an odd generator g. Any odd (ZP_kappa) sequence containing a long zero-sum has length <=2p+2. This is STAB and LS+ at these cells only; the first p congruent to 2 mod3 cell is not covered.
21|4. **LS+2.** At kappa=p-2, p>=7, every odd (ZP_kappa) sequence containing a long zero-sum has length <=3p-5=3kappa+1. The stated E2/E3 families attain this bound. This does not classify every endpoint extremizer (general STAB(p,p-2) is not proved).
22|5. **LS-lin.** For e>=3 and p>=6e-10, LS(p,kappa) holds. Together with the accepted e=2 cell, the linear band is e<=(p+10)/6, always restricted to the new kappa range above.
23|6. **CT.** For prime p>=5, 0<=kappa<=p-1, odd (ZP_kappa) Y, and 1<=n<=|Y| with |Y|-n even in [2kappa+2,2p], sum_x min(n,v_x(Y)) <= p+n-2.
24|7. **CTgen.** For prime p>=5, (p-3)/2<=kappa<=p-1, N=|Y|>=2kappa+3, odd (ZP_kappa) Y containing a long zero-sum: the involution p has multiplicity <=N-2kappa-2 and some odd generator g has multiplicity >=N-2kappa-1. At N=3kappa+2 this supplies v_g>=kappa+1 to the restricted ROW dichotomy; it is not itself a proof of LS+.
25|

23|## Parent derivation
24|
25|Assume an unbalanced longest Z, so S(N)=n_++2pj with j≥1 because 0≤n_+≤2t<2p. Every integer weight in [0,W+n_+] is realized by a submultiset of P=(units of Z)∪Y′ (REALIZE's proof interval plus units). Prefixes N_0 of N with S(N_0)≤m+n_+ give actual zero-sums with available units, so f(N_0)=S(N_0)+|N_0|≤2t. Prefixes with 2p−W≤S(N_0)≤2p+n_+ give the occurrence-disjoint zero-sum (N−N_0)∪X with X⊆P of weight n_++2p−S(N_0); since |X|≥weight−e′, f(N_0)≥2p−e′. The first prefix crossing m+n_+ increases by s≤slack and lands in the second window because W+m≥2L≥2p+2. Combining the two bounds gives L≤2t, contradicting L≥2t+1. ROOM's W-bound uses only longestness; balance gives E=e′+n_+−n_−≤k−1. In ROT-ZB+, DAV and 2kappa^+k≤2p−1 give kappa^≤(2p−2)/3, so S1-sharp supplies (S1); SHgen(H) is 2p−2−2kappa^≥k−1≥E and SHgen(K) uses KETA's k-free inequality with k≤p−1. The corollary's F≥2o_F−1 is equivalent to 2kappa(w)+o_F≤2p−1 and transfers to every admissible normal form; deletion cases preserve both conditions.
26|

1|# Ordered Olson sequence lemma: general LS on the new interval (G-LS) and the original-domain union
2|
3|Recorded at 2026-10-19T09:45:44-0700.
4|
5|**CORRECTNESS_ACCEPTED_SCOPED_PARTIAL, conditional on one declared external statement-level theorem.** G-LS holds as stated below, relative to the parent-accepted internal interfaces at their original scopes and, for e=p-kappa>=3 only, to Lev 2022 Theorem 2 (arXiv:2110.01081v3, Theorem 2) as a premise whose proof was not replayed here. Cells with e=2 (and therefore every cell with p=7) use no Lev input. This is a sequence-level written result. It is not LS+, STAB, INV*, ordered Olson or a word-level theorem. No Lean, human-referee or novelty clearance.
6|
7|## Exact accepted statements
8|
9|1. **G-LS.** Let p>=7 be prime and floor(2p/3)+1<=kappa<=p-2. For every sequence Y of odd elements of C_(2p) with |Y|>=p+2kappa+1 and no zero-sum (occurrence) subsequence of length in [2kappa+2,2p], every zero-sum subsequence of Y has length <=2kappa.
10|2. **Corollary LS-ALL (range union).** LS(p,kappa) holds on the entire original LS definition (/Users/jinleic/repos/aifours/mathematics/evidence/six-lane-20261018-96h/olson/evidence/LS-scope-v1.md lines 9-15): every prime p>=5 and 0<=kappa<=p-2. Proof: accepted S1-sharp ([verdict](/Users/jinleic/repos/aifours/institute/runs/six-lane-2026-10-18-96h/review/olson-s1sharp-parent-verdict-v1.md) `bd4a0a60cdea0765517ca33a9a2ddefb664b24fad7b20e709835bbdec1e94f41`, items 1) gives the same implication with no length hypothesis for 0<=kappa<=floor(2p/3), and G-LS covers floor(2p/3)+1<=kappa<=p-2 for p>=7. At p=5, floor(2p/3)=3=p-2, so S1-sharp alone covers every original cell. Both reviewers independently answered YES with no missing endpoint. The two blocks have different trust bases: e>=3 cells of G-LS also rest on the Lev statement.

30|
31|- **m1, AP' authority.** The δ verdict `ac8ead13b89b4d89dc62d13fd2a20dbf7957b1b427be46bf7e8db5bbb8d5a4c5` item 2 accepts Proposition I-small "with corrected AP-prime proof" but does not list Lemma AP' standalone. **Parent admits Lemma AP' standalone** at its literal scope: for prime p, n>=1 and 3n+2<p, if an (n+1)-term progression with nonzero difference x lies in an arc of size <=2n+1 of Z_p, then x is in {1,-1,2,-2}. Text: statement proof-LS-caseI-smalldelta-v1.md lines 30-31; proof D1 of erratum-1-delta-APprime.md lines 7-17, which supersedes the v1 proof per erratum-2-delta-consolidated.md D-2 m2.3. Parent derivation: with d=|x~|<=(p-1)/2, the n points with rho>=1 lie in I∩(I+x) of size max(0,l-d)+max(0,l-p+d). Case d>=l gives an empty intersection. Case p-l<d<l gives 2l-p<=4n+2-p<n. Otherwise d<=l-n<=n+1, and the integer lift stays in one window because p>3n+1 and l+n<p, so nd<=l-1<=2n and d<=2. Claude re-derived D1 independently and Codex accepted the interface.
32|- **m2, citations.** Where proof section 0 and the package table disagree on line ranges, the governing manifest v2 ranges control. No mathematical effect.
33|- **m3, mandatory wording overlay at proof lines 184 and 187.** Read: "among integers congruent to -j (mod p), only -j and p-j can lie in [-p+h+1,P], which is contained in (-p,p); and p-j>h>P." At j=1 the old open interval (-p,p-1) omitted p-1, but the candidate list was already a valid superset; the conclusion tau=-j is unchanged.
34|- **O1, optional, not adopted.** Claude notes that SLZ3 and minimality of T are dispensable because any long T gives j<=floor((p-2e+1)/2) and still p-j>h. The frozen text remains in scope; no change is needed.

36|## External theorem and parent primary re-read
37|
38|Parent re-read [arXiv:2110.01081v3](https://arxiv.org/html/2110.01081v3) (HTML, dated 01 Jun 2022) at this verdict. Theorem 2: "Suppose that α=(a1,…,an) is a zero-sum-free sequence of elements of an abelian group. If |Σ(α)|<2n then …" there are a and integers 1=x1<=…<=xn with x(k+1)<=x1+…+xk, a_k=x_k a, Σ(α)={0,a,…,(x1+…+xn)a}, and x1+…+xn<ord(a) for finite order. Σ(α) includes the empty subsequence. **No length hypothesis appears**; the paper states the conclusion holds when the sum set is small "not necessarily because 'the whole group is exhausted'". This closes Claude's load-bearing check: at (p,kappa,delta')=(19,13,1) the core has n=9<p/2, and a half-order hypothesis (Theorem 1, Savchev-Chen/Yuan) would not have covered it. The proof consumes only the hypothesis and the structure/interval conclusion; a≠0, ord a=p, n<=S and the occurrence-level coefficient interval are derived in the proof. Lev's proof is not replayed, and the local transcription hash is not a remote byte hash.

1|# Ordered Olson: sharp odd-sequence lemma and uniform restricted dihedral consequence
2|
3|Recorded at 2026-10-19T00:33:24-0700.
4|
5|**CORRECTNESS-ACCEPTED SCOPED PARTIAL, relative to declared classical imports.** Lemma S1-sharp holds for every prime p≥5 and integer 0≤κ≤floor(2p/3). Consequently the accepted dihedral INV*/ordered-Olson route now covers **every prime p≥5** under **3o_F≤2p+1**, with no extra side condition on F. The previous terminal gap (p,k,κhat)=(31,21,20) is closed. This does not prove unrestricted INV*, ordered Olson for all D_2p, or ordered Olson for all finite groups.
6|
7|## Exact statements
8|
9|1. For any sequence Y of odd elements of Z_(2p), if Y has no zero-sum subsequence of length in [2κ+2,2p], then every zero-sum subsequence has length≤2κ, provided0≤κ≤floor(2p/3). No bound on the length of Y is assumed. The threshold is sharp: the implication fails at every κ in [floor(2p/3)+1,p] by the explicit F1 family; κ≥p+1 is not a failure claim.
10|2. Every length-(4p−2) avoider over D_2p with3o_F≤2p+1 satisfies INV* (rotations in one nonzero pair and constant maximal reflection runs). Here o_F counts odd maximal cyclic constant reflection blocks, not arbitrary reflection runs.
11|3. Every length-(4p−1) word over D_2p with3o_F≤2p+1 has an identity2p-subword in increasing index order.
12|

21|## Independent parent derivation
22|
23|Induct onκ, using the accepted length-free S1+ forκ≤(p+1)/2. Setu=2κ=p+δ. A shortest long zero-sumT has evenN≥2p+2; every proper nonempty zero-sum lies in[l,u], l=N−u, and is minimal because a nested split would forceN≤3κ≤2p. Applying the induction toT itself forces a properQ of lengthu; Q′ has lengthl andD=u−l≥0.
24|
25|The imported index-one theorem applies toQ over the composite cyclic groupC_(2p), since|Q|≥p+3. Its basis element has order2p: orders1/p give even elements, order2 would force length2. ThusQ has positive odd coefficients summing2p. WritingQ=g^m S gives mass(S)=p−δ, m≥(p+3δ)/2 andb_min≤p−δ+1.
26|
27|For occurrence-submultisetsQ1⊆Q,W⊆Q′ with equal coefficient sum modulo2p, excluding exactly the empty/full exchange cases, (Q−Q1)W is a proper zero-sum; its length gives |W|≤|Q1|≤|W|+D. The Step6 inclusion-minimalZ0 construction produces aQ1 with too many terms for anyQ′coefficient≥D+2: the |Z0|=1 and |Z0|≥2 inequalities separately contradict the mass bounds. IfQ′coefficient sum≥4p, a first crossing of2p and its predecessor give mass(W)≥2p and≤2p−l+D, forcingp≤3δ−4, impossible. HenceQ′also has coefficient sum2p.
28|
29|The same exchange forbids representingb_min as two or moreQ′terms. Thus t′<b_min and its low nonunit terms have total≤b_min−t′−1. The exact identity Σ_Y(c−3)/2=t′−base, with integralbase=(3l−2p)/2≥3, excludes t′≤2 and permits at most oneYterm above t′. Therefore l≤b_min, contradicting l≥p−δ+2>b_min. This closes the induction without the previous S1(g) Steps3–8 or the exploratory search.
30|
31|For the dihedral consequence, GRX givesκhat≥0 andDAV givesκhat≤k−1≤o_F−1, hence3κhat≤2p−2. GRX supplies(ZP), the newlemma supplies(S1), and3k≤2p+1 supplies the accepted ZX terminal criterion. Restricted REFL applies sinceo_F≤p. The accepted ordered deletion ledger preserves the odd-block bound, andRGR-LONG finishes length4p−1. The former F-dependent alternative existed only to forceκhat into the olderS1range, so it is no longer needed.
32|
33|## Scope, priority and preserved failures
34|
35|New dihedral coverage begins atp≥31; p≤29 coverage was already accepted. The exact old terminal-pair list is discharged; the worker's Q1a/Q1b residual terminology is planning context, not a newly audited completeness claim. Cases outside3o_F≤2p+1 and reflection rigidity ato_F>p remain open.
36|
37|The classical index-one theorem is imported at its generalC_n scope from the [author-statement record](/Users/jinleic/repos/aifours/institute/runs/six-lane-2026-10-16/review/olson-index-one-primary-statement-parent-v1.md); its2007proofs were not read or formalized here. 6|
7|## Exact statements
8|
9|For a 19x8 array over six symbols with at most one agreement per pair, five columns have profile(4,3,3,3,3,3) and three have profile(4,4,3,3,3,2). Q_c denotes each first-five column's quad, T(r)={c:r in Q_c}, and e counts rows with T empty.
10|
11|- **TheoremA:** e=8 or9 is impossible. Exact pair saturation gives 5 Phi_5(e)+Phi_6(2e)+Phi_9(e)<=binom(e,2); the respective lower bounds29 and38 exceed28 and36.
12|- **TheoremB:** e=7 and two rows whose T-sets have size3 and whose two-element complements are not listed skeleton types is impossible. Each is forced into two pair blocks; their T-sets intersect, so those four pair blocks are distinct, but only three exist.
13|- F1-F5, Lemmas1-6 and the stated rescue implications are accepted at their literal conditions, read through all three parent errata. No unproved diagnostic compatibility or realization is accepted.
14|

14|## Exact setting and parent derivation
15|
16|C = {i*} disjoint-union O, |O|=5. n_d=2^e_d 5^h_d w_d has no 3-part; n_i*=2^e* 3^f* 5^h* w_*; n_c=2·3^f_c 5^h_c w_c for c in O. min(e_d,e*)=2; at most one vertex has f>=2 and at most one has h>=1; the w parts are pairwise coprime and prime to30. Pairwise gcds are6 inside C,4 on d–i*,2 on d–O.
17|
18|The parent independently checked the following written argument before the reviews and checked the editorial repairs afterwards:
19|1. At d, the six local indices have pairwise gcd3 unless the d–i* entry is3 and the 3-marker is in O. Uniform weight1/3 gives 5/6+a/12<1; a=2 is impossible. INV and a+a*<=4 ensure at least one orientation has a=1. Invert first if necessary, then normalise g_d=1.
20|2. At each O-vertex, pairwise incompatibility and feasible local weights exclude O–O entry3 and three entries1. When h_d>=1, they also exclude entry5; inverse entries1 handle four fives. These facts hold in both orientations without circularity.
21|3. Intersect the conjugated O-subgroups, marked vertices first. Each subsequent unmarked index is6w_e; the multiplier k in T_(m+1)=lcm(n_e,T_m)k satisfies k<=alpha*_ej<=5. No prime greater than5 enters except the pairwise-coprime w factors. If h_d>=1, all O-indices and multipliers are5-free. Consequently gcd(n_d,T*_O)=2^min(e_d,v2(T*_O)).
22|4. The six other cosets meet the U_d-fibres with total incidence11n_d/4. Each fibre holds at most3 pieces by Sun(4). The M fibres in W*_O U_d meet none of the five O-cosets, and U_d meets no other coset, so 11n_d/4 <=3(n_d-M)+(M-1), yielding M<n_d/8.
23|5. Lagrange and the subgroup product formula give M>=n_d/gcd(n_d,T*_O). Thus gcd(n_d,T*_O)>8, forcing e_d>=4 and e*=2. R0 preserves the conjugated intersections in a finite quotient and extends the result to arbitrary groups.
24|

18|## Independent parent derivation
19|
20|At the 2-marked centre z, all neighbour cofactors are odd and 3-free, and at most one carries a 5-marker. LFC depends on neighbour labels, not the apex's own cofactor. Thus ROW-U applies directly when all neighbours are unmarked; otherwise the explicitly stated ROW-1-deg transfer applies. The threes are excluded by adding a weight on the 5-marked neighbour to the five-neighbour value 5. An all-five row is excluded by applying LFC to the inverse row of ones. Both alpha rows at z therefore lie in {1,2,4}, contain at most one 1, and have at least three 4s. INV makes the two sets of 4-neighbours complementary triples F and F*.
21|
22|For a cross pair a in F*, b in F, the triangle has values (s,4,x), with s in {1,2}. Direct evaluation of the accepted T bounds gives tau_min = lcm(4,x/gcd(x,r_z)) and tau_max = min(sx,4 min(s,x),s min(4,x)). Values x=1,3 are impossible. A possible x=5 forces inverse x*=1, also impossible. Hence (x,x*) is one of (2,2),(2,4),(4,2). If s=1, x=4. For surviving values, tau_min=4; F7 gives X_a subset X_b exactly in the needed cases xs<=4. The inverse containment has the reversed roles, with no apex error.
23|
24|The true VFC vector is q=alpha*. On tight centre edges alpha+alpha*=6, D_v=Y_v by equality of finite measures. Since neighbour cofactors are odd and 3-free, the VFC local gcds reduce to those of the values 1,2,4. Two light indices and one heavy index cannot share a right coset by Sun(3); a local index labelled 1 is coprime to every other and must stand alone by Sun(2). Their total union avoids U_z and has measure strictly below 1.
25|
26|The possible ones in the two centre rows give four exhaustive types. If both exist, CROSS forces (4,4), impossible. If exactly one exists, F7 and tightness place its nonempty D-set inside a heavy D-set despite coprime local indices, impossible; inversion handles the other orientation. If no ones exist, every light vertex having an incident cross-2 edge has a D-set disjoint from every other light D-set. At most one remaining light vertex would make three sets of measure 1/3 pairwise disjoint, contradicting the strict union bound. Thus both orientations have an out vertex; their crossing edge is 4 in both alpha matrices, again impossible.
27|

16|## Accepted statements and parent derivation
17|
18|1. **LFC, k≤7.** For disjoint left cosets and a fixed vertex v, lambda_j=[U_v:U_v∩U_j]=n_j alpha_vj/g_vj, and g_jU_j meets t_j=n_v alpha_vj/g_vj left U_v-fibres. A common point p writes each intersection as p(U_j∩U_v); after translating into U_v these are disjoint left cosets with those lambda indices. At most six pieces share any nonexclusive fibre. Sun(t),2≤t≤6, applied to every subfamily gives the hereditary allowed-set condition. Summing feasible weights over the n_v−1 fibres gives sum y_j alpha_vj/g_vj≤1−1/n_v<1. No conjugation is missing from this left-fibre argument.
19|2. **INV.** Invert each coset and write it as g_i^−1(g_iU_i g_i^−1). This preserves disjointness and the labelled index tuple. With h=g_v^−1g_j, the double coset U_v h U_j has measure alpha*_vj/g_vj. It is disjoint from U_vU_j, so alpha_vj+alpha*_vj≤g_vj. The original VFC(V1) defines q by that same double-coset measure, hence q=alpha*. The parent directly read VFC(V1) and the index-defined class definitions; same-class assertions do not extend to classes defined using additional unpreserved data.
20|3. **ROW-U.** In K7, a vertex with six unmarked neighbours has only alpha∈{1,2,4}, at most one 1 and at least three 4s. Pairwise-coprime w_j prime to30 reduce fibre gcds to gcds of alpha≤5. The R1/R24/R3/R5 feasible weights in the candidate cover every hereditary subset; for N entries of R1∪R24 the value is at least min(N,4). Every excluded row gives a value≥6, contradicting LFC.
21|4. **K7-M0.** If all seven indices are 6w_i, with w_i pairwise coprime and prime to30, ROW-U applies globally to alpha and alpha*. Each vertex has at least three alpha=4 and at least three alpha*=4 edges, disjoint by INV. Thus exactly three alpha=4 edges meet every vertex. Symmetry would give a 3-regular graph on seven vertices, with odd degree sum21, impossible. This excludes the previously open all-2 singleton pattern and the entire unmarked Q24 branch, not only a bounded range.
22|5. **K63-U0.** Six indices 6w_i and one 3w_d, all w pairwise coprime and prime to30, cannot occur. If some C–d alpha=1, the d fibre contributes1/3 and the five other C-neighbours contribute at least4/6, contradicting LFC. Therefore all C–d alpha=2. At d the six local indices are4w_i; weights1/4 give total1, another contradiction.
23|6. **Arbitrary groups.** The intersection of the finitely many subgroup cores is normal, finite-index and contained in every subgroup. The quotient preserves indices, intersections and coset disjointness; normality also preserves the conjugated intersections in INV. Therefore the finite arguments lift to the stated arbitrary-group scopes.
